| Its own word | What it means |
|---|---|
| *appreciation* | Appreciation, in the group's definition, joins sensitive awareness to an increase of value. |
| *entitlement* | Entitlement is the 'to me' opposite of appreciation: feeling owed 'what’s mine' and resenting when it is not acknowledged. |
| *keys to mastery* | The keys to mastery are the four rules that an appreciation be sincere, unarguable, specific, and succinct. |

### Owning your victimhood

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Type | a move |
| When the coach uses it | The coach uses it with a person who is overwhelmed and is denying that they feel like a victim. |
| What the coach does | Larson describes a client whose life was unraveling, and her own collapse as a caregiver, and a turn in which, in her words, she 'surrendered to the victimhood I had been avoiding owning'. The practice asks a person facing an overwhelming situation whether they are willing simply to acknowledge that they feel 'at the effect' of it, to feel how deeply they feel that without judgment, to check whether approval, security, or control feels most threatened, and to offer themselves compassion. If the answer to any question is no, the person acknowledges the resistance and the story they tell about why the feeling is not allowed. |
| Steps | (1) The person thinks of a situation that feels hard or overwhelming. (2) The person asks: 'Are you willing to simply acknowledge that you feel at the effect of this person or situation?' (3) If the answer is yes, the person asks whether they are willing to embrace just how deeply they feel at the effect, without judgment or resistance. (4) The person checks: 'Is approval, security, or control feeling most at threat?' (5) The person asks whether they are willing to offer themselves loving compassion for being human and feeling exactly as they do. (6) If any answer is no, the person acknowledges the resistance and the story they are telling themselves about why it is not okay to feel what they feel. |
| What it does | The move has a person admit, without judgment, that they feel at the mercy of a situation before any attempt to shift. |
| What is facilitated | The move makes it possible for a person to admit a state that the group's model otherwise asks them to leave, which, Larson writes, lets the nervous system 'exhale'; the admission is the person's. The coach supplies questions and permission, not a shift. |
| Who decides | B, the facilitator shapes the process; the people supply the content. The coach supplies the questions and the permission, and the person decides what they are willing to acknowledge. |
| The evidence | The source is one coach's account of a client and of herself; the sources opened contain no study. |
| How it bears on the book (the researcher's reading) | Protection and defense: The move meets the defended 'to me' state with acknowledgment and compassion rather than with a demand to shift. Whether the cause lies in the person or the system: The example includes a displaced family and an intimidating chief executive, and the client's relief came partly through leaving the job, which acknowledges causes outside the person. |
| Where it differs from the book (the researcher's reading) | This 2025 practice is nearer the book's reading of contraction as protection than the group's founding instruction to shift above the line; it still ends in the group's aim of choosing a new way of being. |
| Quoted from its sources | "I surrendered to the victimhood I had been avoiding owning" (https://conscious.is/blog/the-unexpected-power-of-owning-your-victimhood/) "Are you willing to simply acknowledge that you feel at the effect of this person or situation?" (https://conscious.is/blog/the-unexpected-power-of-owning-your-victimhood/) "just how deeply you feel at the effect, without judgement or resistance" (https://conscious.is/blog/the-unexpected-power-of-owning-your-victimhood/) "Is approval, security, or control feeling most at threat?" (https://conscious.is/blog/the-unexpected-power-of-owning-your-victimhood/) "loving compassion —for being human, having a human experience, and feeling exactly as you do" (https://conscious.is/blog/the-unexpected-power-of-owning-your-victimhood/) "the story you’re telling yourself about why it’s not okay to feel what you feel" (https://conscious.is/blog/the-unexpected-power-of-owning-your-victimhood/) "It’s what allows your nervous system to exhale, your heart to soften, and your mind to see clearly." (https://conscious.is/blog/the-unexpected-power-of-owning-your-victimhood/) "Not to force a fix, but to choose a new way of being with it all, exactly as it was." (https://conscious.is/blog/the-unexpected-power-of-owning-your-victimhood/) "Her doctor and therapist advised immediate medical leave." (https://conscious.is/blog/the-unexpected-power-of-owning-your-victimhood/) |
| Date | 2025 (Larson's post, April 2025) |
| In the vault | `notes/suffering is contraction never corruption.md`; The vault's doctrine reads a contraction as protection that can open, not as a fault. Larson's practice moves the group's model toward that reading by acknowledging the defended state first, while the group's founding texts pair each commitment with its 'to me' opposite to be left behind. |
| Sources | https://conscious.is/blog/the-unexpected-power-of-owning-your-victimhood/ |
| Verified at those sources | yes |

| Its own word | What it means |
|---|---|
| *at the effect* | Being 'at the effect' is the group's phrase for experiencing oneself as caused by other people, circumstances, or conditions. |
| *approval, security, control* | Approval, security, and control are three wants the group asks a person to source within themselves rather than from outside. |

## Peter Crone

| Move | Type | When the coach uses it | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| The prisons of the mind | a theory | Crone states this account when he explains his work to an interviewer or opens a coaching conversation. | The account tells the client that their suffering comes from a self-belief formed from past hurt, so that belief becomes what the coaching examines. |
| Reverse engineering a symptom to its prison | a move | Crone uses the move at the start of a coaching conversation, when a person has described what they want help with. | The move traces a presenting problem back to a self-belief the coach names. |
| Investigating the validity of the constraint | a move | Crone uses the move after the person has named the belief he has traced their problem to. | The move has the person check whether a self-belief is a fact about them and see that it is a learned sentence. |
| Who could you be in the absence of that constraint? | a move | Crone uses the question right after a person has recognized that a self-belief is not true. | The question has the person picture who they would be without the belief. |
| Changing 'I am depressed' to 'I feel depressed' | a move | Crone uses the move when a client describes a symptom or diagnosis as their identity. | The move has the person restate a symptom as something they feel rather than something they are. |
| Do you know what's going to happen in five minutes? | a move | Crone uses the move when a client, often an athlete before a game, predicts failure from past results. | The move has the client admit they do not know the future, which removes the certainty from their fear. |
| Making space: the maternal energy of acceptance | a move | Crone uses the move at the start of work with every client and whenever fear or anxiety comes up. | The move has the coach accept the person's feeling first, so the person feels held before any belief is questioned. |
| Reflecting a client's phrasing as perception, not truth | a move | Crone uses the move when a client states a judgment about someone or something as a fact. | The move has the client hear their description as their own perception. |
| Awareness, then practice | a technique | Crone uses this sequence across a coaching engagement, from the first session to the months after. | The sequence has the person first see through a belief and then practice living without it. |
| Humor and tough love | a move | Crone uses the move with performers and athletes he has coached for some time, when worry about results is getting in their way. | The move has the coach bluntly redirect a client from worry about outcomes to care for the work. |

### The prisons of the mind

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Type | a theory |
| When the coach uses it | Crone states this account when he explains his work to an interviewer or opens a coaching conversation. |
| What the coach does | Crone distinguishes pain, which he treats as physical, from suffering, which he calls psychological and emotional and 'not necessary'. He holds that early hurts leave a person believing sentences about themselves, such as 'I'm not enough' or 'I'm not loved', and that these beliefs act as 'prisons', the views through which the person sees life and from which coping strategies such as perfectionism and people-pleasing follow. He says most people are defined by two or three such primary prisons, that the mind's job is to predict and protect, so that 'past hurt informs future fear', and that a person's 'true inherent state is freedom'. |
| What it does | The account tells the client that their suffering comes from a self-belief formed from past hurt, so that belief becomes what the coaching examines. |
| What is facilitated | On Crone's account the client's seeing that a self-belief is 'not a truth' ends the suffering tied to it; Crone supplies the diagnosis of which prison is operating and the questions, and the client supplies the recognition that the belief is not a fact. |
| Who decides | not applicable |
| The evidence | No published study of Crone's method was found; the support offered is his own account and client testimonials, and his site states that he 'does not claim to diagnose or treat any specific conditions.' |
| How it bears on the book (the researcher's reading) | Whether the cause lies in the person or the system: Crone places the cause of anxiety and depression in beliefs formed in childhood and contrasts his work with 'strategizing and perfecting people's circumstances'. Recognition: Crone describes freedom as the person's inherent state, uncovered when a false self-belief is seen through, which resembles the book's account of coming to know what was already there. |
| Where it differs from the book (the researcher's reading) | Crone locates suffering in beliefs formed from past hurt and calls it unnecessary once the belief is seen through, while the book treats contraction as having a function and keeps structural causes in view. The corpus quotes him as saying 'you are living inside a sentence you didn't choose'; that sentence was not found in the two transcripts read, although his account there, of spoken self-negations formed in childhood, matches it in substance. |
| Limits and critiques | Crone describes therapy and medication as not resolving 'anything at the root cause' and tells of listeners whose skin conditions cleared after hearing him ('To me, it's just physics'), while his own site says he 'does not claim to diagnose or treat any specific conditions'; the gap between the claims in interviews and the disclaimer is the main documented risk. |
| Quoted from its sources | "suffering is psychological and emotional. It is part of the human experience, but it is nonetheless not necessary" (https://miraclemorning.com/peter-crone/) "So, these prisons are the lenses through which we view life, and hence how we react and how we act." (https://miraclemorning.com/peter-crone/) "most humans are sort of defined by typically two or three primary prisons" (https://miraclemorning.com/peter-crone/) "what we're speaking at the deepest level is some negation of ourself. I'm not enough, I'm not loved." (https://miraclemorning.com/peter-crone/) "past hurt informs future fear" (https://www.onecommune.com/blog/the-mind-architect-with-peter-crone) "Our true inherent state is freedom." (https://miraclemorning.com/peter-crone/) "When we dissolve the prisons, then suffering ends." (https://miraclemorning.com/peter-crone/) "I don't solve problems. I dissolve them." (https://www.petercrone.com/) "which is of course why people are in therapy for decades, or they still have to stay on whatever pharmaceutical product they're taking, because it's not actually resolving anything at the root cause." (https://www.onecommune.com/blog/the-mind-architect-with-peter-crone) "To me, it's just physics, right?" (https://www.onecommune.com/blog/the-mind-architect-with-peter-crone) "Peter Crone does not claim to diagnose or treat any specific conditions." (https://www.petercrone.com/benefits-disclaimer) "If all that happened is that today Jeff has a little less anxiety then the world has a little less anxiety." (https://www.onecommune.com/blog/the-mind-architect-with-peter-crone) "So my work is less in strategizing and perfecting people's circumstances, which a lot of experts do." (https://www.onecommune.com/blog/the-mind-architect-with-peter-crone) |
| Date | Stated in an interview published by Commune on 12 June 2020; restated in an interview of 9 July 2025 |
| In the vault | `notes/the transformation field divides into five theories of where suffering lives.md`; The note gives Crone one clause, the corpus's quotation 'you are living inside a sentence you didn't choose'; the vault has no note on him and the declines list records him as not needing one. His own words, read here, name the belief as a 'prison' and the aim as freedom. |
| Sources | https://www.onecommune.com/blog/the-mind-architect-with-peter-crone; https://miraclemorning.com/peter-crone/; https://www.petercrone.com/; https://www.petercrone.com/benefits-disclaimer |
| Verified at those sources | yes |

| Its own word | What it means |
|---|---|
| *prison* | Crone's word for a belief about oneself, formed early, that limits how a person sees and acts |
| *primal prisons* | the small set of basic self-negating beliefs, such as not being enough, that Crone says underlie most suffering (a 2025 show page lists ten) |
| *dissolve rather than solve* | Crone's claim that he removes the belief beneath a problem instead of treating the problem |
| *past hurt informs future fear* | Crone's summary of how a remembered injury becomes anxiety about what has not happened |

### Reverse engineering a symptom to its prison

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Type | a move |
| When the coach uses it | Crone uses the move at the start of a coaching conversation, when a person has described what they want help with. |
| What the coach does | Crone listens to the problem a person brings, such as anxiety, a weight or health issue, a failing relationship, or trouble making money, and works back from it to the self-belief he takes to be driving it. He then leads the person to discover that belief, often a sentence heard in childhood. In one example he gives, a woman trying to improve her coaching business found that her father had told her she would 'turn out to be a loser like her mom and her sister', which Crone took to be driving her perfectionism. |
| Steps | (1) The person describes a present problem or symptom. (2) Crone listens to the story and identifies the underlying 'prison' ('once I hear the story, I have ... the ability to suddenly know what prison they're stuck in'). (3) Crone asks questions that lead the person back to the originating belief or event. (4) The person names the belief, for example that something was their fault or that they are flawed. |
| What it does | The move traces a presenting problem back to a self-belief the coach names. |
| What is facilitated | The move makes it easier for the person to bring into view a belief they had not noticed was shaping their choices; Crone supplies the identification of which belief it is. |
| Who decides | D, the facilitator directs or interprets with authority. Crone says that on hearing the story he knows which prison the person is in, so the diagnosis comes from him. |
| The evidence | No published study of Crone's method was found; the support offered is his own account and client testimonials, and his site states that he 'does not claim to diagnose or treat any specific conditions.' |
| How it bears on the book (the researcher's reading) | Who decides the content: Crone says he knows the person's prison from their story, so the naming of the cause comes from the coach rather than from the person. Being seen and recognized by another: The move depends on the client feeling that Crone has seen accurately what drives them. |
| Where it differs from the book (the researcher's reading) | The book leaves the naming of what is wrong to the reader and treats any diagnosis as provisional, while this move rests on the coach's confident identification of the belief. |
| Limits and critiques | A single belief identified by the coach can be wrong or incomplete, and the move has no stated check for that; Crone describes the identification as coming 'for whatever crazy, magical purposes, reasons'. |
| Quoted from its sources | "So, awareness is to investigate where are you stuck, and I reverse engineer." (https://miraclemorning.com/peter-crone/) "So, once I hear the story, I have, for whatever crazy, magical purposes, reasons, the ability to suddenly know what prison they're stuck in." (https://miraclemorning.com/peter-crone/) "you can't be held accountable for that which you're oblivious to" (https://miraclemorning.com/peter-crone/) "her dad had told her one day that she's going to turn out to be a loser like her mom and her sister." (https://miraclemorning.com/peter-crone/) |
| Date | Stated in an interview published on Hal Elrod's Achieve Your Goals podcast page, 9 July 2025 |
| In the vault | `notes/the transformation field divides into five theories of where suffering lives.md`; The vault holds only the corpus's summary of Crone; this move is recorded here for the first time. |
| Sources | https://miraclemorning.com/peter-crone/; https://www.petercrone.com/benefits-disclaimer |
| Verified at those sources | yes |

| Its own word | What it means |
|---|---|
| *reverse engineer* | Crone's term for working back from a present symptom to the belief he says produces it |
| *awareness* | the first stage of his work, bringing an unconscious belief into view, which he ties to Carl Jung's saying about making the unconscious conscious |

### Investigating the validity of the constraint

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Type | a move |
| When the coach uses it | Crone uses the move after the person has named the belief he has traced their problem to. |
| What the coach does | Once a belief such as 'it's my fault' has been named, Crone asks the person whether it is actually true. His signature question asks whether, if he cut the person open, he would find a manufacturing label saying the belief is part of them. He then asks where the belief lives and what it is made of, leading the person from beliefs to words and, in his account, to sound, so that the person sees the belief as spoken language rather than a fact about themselves. |
| Steps | (1) Crone restates the named belief and asks whether it is really true. (2) Crone asks: 'if I cut you open, am I going to find a manufacturing label that says in this place, Kenny, it's his fault, right?' (3) When the person says no, Crone asks where the belief lives and 'what's it made up of?' (4) Crone leads the person from 'beliefs' to 'words' and then to sound, so the belief is seen as something spoken. (5) The person recognizes that the belief is not a truth, which Crone calls 'the euphoric moment'. |
| What it does | The move has the person check whether a self-belief is a fact about them and see that it is a learned sentence. |
| What is facilitated | The questions make it easier for a person to see that a self-belief is a sentence they learned rather than a fact about them; the person supplies the answer that no such label exists. |
| Who decides | B, the facilitator shapes the process; the people supply the content |
| The evidence | No published study of Crone's method was found; the support offered is his own account and client testimonials, and his site states that he 'does not claim to diagnose or treat any specific conditions.' |
| How it bears on the book (the researcher's reading) | Knowing that something happened: Crone describes visible changes when the person sees through the belief ('the eyes, the breathing patterns change'), which he treats as the sign that the belief has gone. Recognition: The move aims at the person seeing that a belief was never part of them, which resembles the book's un-forgetting. |
| Where it differs from the book (the researcher's reading) | The move treats the self-belief as false and its falsity as enough to end it, while the book treats a contraction as something that has a function and releases over time. |
| Limits and critiques | Crone reports that at this moment 'the physiology starts to heal', a health claim his site's disclaimer does not make. |
| Quoted from its sources | "if I cut you open, am I going to find a manufacturing label that says in this place, Kenny, it's his fault, right?" (https://miraclemorning.com/peter-crone/) "What's in my head? Okay, what's it made up of? People are like, oh, well, beliefs. And I'm like, well, yeah, what are beliefs? What's ultimately words?" (https://miraclemorning.com/peter-crone/) "the dissolution process as I spoke to earlier, is really to investigate the validity of that self-perception or of that perspective that you have" (https://www.onecommune.com/blog/the-mind-architect-with-peter-crone) "That's when the floodgates go on and the eyes, the breathing patterns change, the physiology starts to heal." (https://miraclemorning.com/peter-crone/) |
| Date | Stated in an interview published on Hal Elrod's Achieve Your Goals podcast page, 9 July 2025; the same inquiry is described in the interview of 12 June 2020 |
| In the vault | `notes/the transformation field divides into five theories of where suffering lives.md`; The vault has no record of this move; it parallels Katie's 'Is it true?' (which Crone praised in 2020), and the corpus places both in one camp. |
| Sources | https://miraclemorning.com/peter-crone/; https://www.onecommune.com/blog/the-mind-architect-with-peter-crone; https://www.petercrone.com/benefits-disclaimer |
| Verified at those sources | yes |

| Its own word | What it means |
|---|---|
| *validity* | whether a self-belief is actually true, which is what Crone's questions test |
| *manufacturing label* | Crone's image for a fact built into a person, used to show that a belief is not one |

### Who could you be in the absence of that constraint?

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Type | a move |
| When the coach uses it | Crone uses the question right after a person has recognized that a self-belief is not true. |
| What the coach does | After the person has seen that a belief is not true, Crone asks who they could be, and what could become available to them, without it. He reports that at this point people's eyes and breathing change and they say they feel light and could do whatever they want. The question moves the person from seeing through the belief to imagining a life no longer organized around it. |
| Steps | (1) The person has just recognized that a self-belief is not a truth. (2) Crone asks: 'who could you be and what could become available for you in the absence of that constraint?' (3) The person describes what opens up, and Crone watches for changes in eyes and breathing. |
| What it does | The question has the person picture who they would be without the belief. |
| What is facilitated | The question makes it easier for the person to imagine and feel their life without the belief; the person supplies the picture. |
| Who decides | B, the facilitator shapes the process; the people supply the content |
| The evidence | No published study of Crone's method was found; the support offered is his own account and client testimonials, and his site states that he 'does not claim to diagnose or treat any specific conditions.' |
| How it bears on the book (the researcher's reading) | The future: The question turns the person toward what could become possible once the belief no longer limits them. Recognition: The question asks who the person is without an acquired belief, which is close in form to the book's promise that a reader recognizes what they already are. |
| Where it differs from the book (the researcher's reading) | Crone asks the question in a live conversation right after his diagnosis, while the book leaves the reader to arrive at such a question without a coach. |
| Quoted from its sources | "And then I say, who could you be and what could become available for you in the absence of that constraint?" (https://miraclemorning.com/peter-crone/) "That's when the floodgates go on and the eyes, the breathing patterns change, the physiology starts to heal." (https://miraclemorning.com/peter-crone/) "Who would you be without the thought?" (https://thework.com/wp-content/uploads/2019/02/English_LB.pdf) |
| Date | Stated in an interview published on Hal Elrod's Achieve Your Goals podcast page, 9 July 2025 |
| In the vault | `notes/the transformation field divides into five theories of where suffering lives.md`; The vault has no record of this move. |
| Sources | https://miraclemorning.com/peter-crone/; https://thework.com/wp-content/uploads/2019/02/English_LB.pdf; https://www.petercrone.com/benefits-disclaimer |
| Verified at those sources | yes |

| Its own word | What it means |
|---|---|
| *constraint* | Crone's word for a limiting belief once it has been named |
| *the euphoric moment* | Crone's name for the point at which a person sees that a self-belief is not true |

### Changing 'I am depressed' to 'I feel depressed' (a plain description; the coach gives it no name)

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Type | a move |
| When the coach uses it | Crone uses the move when a client describes a symptom or diagnosis as their identity. |
| What the coach does | When a client says 'I am depressed' or declares a chronic condition as an identity, Crone points out that the sentence makes a symptom into who the person is. He proposes saying 'I feel depressed' instead, which he says leaves 'a little bit more space'. He adds that if depression really were who the person was, they would not experience it as depression, because they feel it only against their true nature, which he calls freedom. |
| Steps | (1) The client describes themselves with 'I am' plus a symptom, such as 'I am depressed.' (2) Crone names the statement as nailing one's foot to the floor around a symptom. (3) Crone proposes the wording 'I feel depressed' and asks whether it leaves more space. (4) Crone adds that the person could not experience depression at all if it were their nature. |
| What it does | The move has the person restate a symptom as something they feel rather than something they are. |
| What is facilitated | The rewording makes it easier for the person to hold a symptom as something they experience rather than as what they are; the person decides whether it fits. |
| Who decides | C, the facilitator offers content as an option. Crone offers the rewording as a better way to speak; the person decides whether to adopt it. |
| The evidence | No published study of Crone's method was found; the support offered is his own account and client testimonials, and his site states that he 'does not claim to diagnose or treat any specific conditions.' |
| How it bears on the book (the researcher's reading) | Remaining the author of one's own change: The move separates the person from the label, which leaves them free to relate to the symptom differently. Harm and its conditions: Crone pairs the move with the claim that calling oneself depressed is 'a complete lie', while saying he does not deny the symptoms; a person with a diagnosed condition could hear this as dismissal of it. |
| Where it differs from the book (the researcher's reading) | The move treats a clinical label as an obstacle to freedom, while the book does not dispute medical descriptions and addresses a reader who may also be in treatment. |
| Quoted from its sources | "But to say I am depressed is such a disservice to that person's ability to find freedom, because they're nailing their foot to the floor around a symptom" (https://www.onecommune.com/blog/the-mind-architect-with-peter-crone) "Versus saying, "I feel depressed," right? Do you see, there's a little bit more space there." (https://www.onecommune.com/blog/the-mind-architect-with-peter-crone) "Now I'm not denying that they don't feel great or they might have symptoms that Western medicine is saying are akin to depression." (https://www.onecommune.com/blog/the-mind-architect-with-peter-crone) "it's a complete lie." (https://www.onecommune.com/blog/the-mind-architect-with-peter-crone) |
| Date | Stated in an interview published by Commune on 12 June 2020 |
| In the vault | `notes/the transformation field divides into five theories of where suffering lives.md`; The vault has no record of this move. |
| Sources | https://www.onecommune.com/blog/the-mind-architect-with-peter-crone; https://www.petercrone.com/benefits-disclaimer |
| Verified at those sources | yes |

| Its own word | What it means |
|---|---|
| *I am versus I feel* | Crone's distinction between naming a symptom as one's identity and naming it as a passing experience |
| *space* | Crone's word for the room a person has around an experience when they do not identify with it |

### Do you know what's going to happen in five minutes?

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Type | a move |
| When the coach uses it | Crone uses the move when a client, often an athlete before a game, predicts failure from past results. |
| What the coach does | When a client predicts a bad outcome, such as an athlete sure a pitcher will strike him out again, Crone asks whether they know what will happen in five minutes and, when they say no, how they can know what will happen hours later. He then offers an opposite prediction, that the athlete goes three for four with a home run, and points out that it is 'as real as the future you just presented'. The move rests on Crone's own discovery, after a breakup, that the answer to all his anxious questions was three words: 'I don't know.' |
| Steps | (1) The client states a feared prediction as if it were fact. (2) Crone acknowledges the feeling: 'Now I'm not denying that you're hurt and you're embarrassed and you're scared.' (3) Crone asks: 'do you know what's going to happen in five minutes?' (4) Crone asks how, then, the client can know what will happen later. (5) Crone offers a hopeful prediction and says it is exactly as real as the feared one. |
| What it does | The move has the client admit they do not know the future, which removes the certainty from their fear. |
| What is facilitated | The move makes it easier for the client to stop treating a fear as a forecast; the coach supplies the questions and the counter-example, and the client supplies the admission that they do not know. |
| Who decides | B, the facilitator shapes the process; the people supply the content |
| The evidence | No published study of Crone's method was found; the support offered is his own account and client testimonials, and his site states that he 'does not claim to diagnose or treat any specific conditions.' |
| How it bears on the book (the researcher's reading) | The future: The move treats predictions of failure as fear rather than knowledge and replaces them with admitted uncertainty. Timing and pacing: Crone first acknowledges the fear and only then questions the prediction. |
| Where it differs from the book (the researcher's reading) | The move uses the unknown future to loosen fear, while the book's arc treats not-knowing as a stage a reader passes through over time rather than as a point the coach establishes in one exchange. |
| Quoted from its sources | "I would introduce you to, do you know what's going to happen in five minutes?" (https://www.onecommune.com/blog/the-mind-architect-with-peter-crone) "What if I told you you actually went three for four and you've got a homer?" (https://www.onecommune.com/blog/the-mind-architect-with-peter-crone) "well, that's as real as the future you just presented to me that you're worried about" (https://www.onecommune.com/blog/the-mind-architect-with-peter-crone) "Now I'm not denying that you're hurt and you're embarrassed and you're scared. That's fine. But that's got nothing to do with what's actually going to happen." (https://www.onecommune.com/blog/the-mind-architect-with-peter-crone) "It was three words: I don't know." (https://www.onecommune.com/blog/the-mind-architect-with-peter-crone) |
| Date | Stated in an interview published by Commune on 12 June 2020 |
| In the vault | `notes/the transformation field divides into five theories of where suffering lives.md`; The vault has no record of this move. |
| Sources | https://www.onecommune.com/blog/the-mind-architect-with-peter-crone; https://www.petercrone.com/benefits-disclaimer |
| Verified at those sources | yes |

| Its own word | What it means |
|---|---|
| *I don't know* | the answer Crone says ended his own anxious questioning and that he treats as the truth about the future |
| *predict and protect* | Crone's description of what the brain does with past hurt |

### Making space: the maternal energy of acceptance

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Type | a move |
| When the coach uses it | Crone uses the move at the start of work with every client and whenever fear or anxiety comes up. |
| What the coach does | Crone says he begins with everyone by accepting the fear or feeling the person has, which he calls 'a feminine approach' and 'the quintessential mother energy', before questioning any belief. He tells an athlete that it is okay to be worried, so that the athlete feels held, safe, and seen. With an anxious flyer he proposes making space for the frightened part of him, 'your quote unquote little boy', rather than trying to calculate away the anxiety, and asks whether he can find 'unconditional acceptance' for the part that is not perfect. |
| Steps | (1) Crone names the feeling and accepts it: 'It's okay that you're worried'. (2) Crone watches for the person to feel held, safe, and seen before going further. (3) Crone invites the person to make space for the frightened part of themselves rather than resolving or solving the feeling. (4) Crone asks whether the person can find 'space, love, unconditional acceptance' for the imperfect part. (5) Only then does Crone question the belief or prediction that drives the fear. |
| What it does | The move has the coach accept the person's feeling first, so the person feels held before any belief is questioned. |
| What is facilitated | The move makes it easier for the person to stop fighting a feeling and to feel safe enough to look at the belief behind it; the coach supplies acceptance, and the person supplies the willingness to let the feeling be. |
| Who decides | B, the facilitator shapes the process; the people supply the content |
| The evidence | No published study of Crone's method was found; the support offered is his own account and client testimonials, and his site states that he 'does not claim to diagnose or treat any specific conditions.' |
| How it bears on the book (the researcher's reading) | The container that holds people while they change: Crone makes acceptance the first condition of every session, before inquiry begins. Being seen and recognized by another: Crone names being seen as one of three effects the acceptance should have, beside feeling held and feeling safe. Protection and defense: The move meets the anxious part with acceptance instead of argument, and treats the effort to control the fear as itself anxious behavior. |
| Where it differs from the book (the researcher's reading) | Crone claims that a feared event 'won't happen because you've allowed it', a promise the book does not make about acceptance. |
| Limits and critiques | The claim that allowing a fear prevents the feared event is stated without evidence and could lead a person to blame themselves if the event happens anyway. |
| Quoted from its sources | "And that's much more a feminine approach, which I would assert is where I start with everyone." (https://www.onecommune.com/blog/the-mind-architect-with-peter-crone) "So the quintessential mother energy allows the feeling to be there." (https://www.onecommune.com/blog/the-mind-architect-with-peter-crone) "I'm bringing this maternal energy of acceptance. It's okay that you're worried that you may not do well against this pitcher. I got it. Now, so then they feel held. They feel safe. They feel seen." (https://www.onecommune.com/blog/the-mind-architect-with-peter-crone) "You allow it to be there versus trying to resolve it or solve it." (https://www.onecommune.com/blog/the-mind-architect-with-peter-crone) "what if we took your quote unquote little boy who's anxious and we made space for him" (https://www.onecommune.com/blog/the-mind-architect-with-peter-crone) "Can you, as a man, find space, love, unconditional acceptance for the part of you that may from time to time not be impeccably perfect in your society?" (https://www.onecommune.com/blog/the-mind-architect-with-peter-crone) "Now, if you find space for that, first of all, whatever you're concerned about won't happen because you've allowed it, you've already made space for it." (https://www.onecommune.com/blog/the-mind-architect-with-peter-crone) |
| Date | Stated in an interview published by Commune on 12 June 2020 |
| In the vault | `notes/joe hudson.md`; The Hudson note records the welcome of a feeling as Hudson's theory; Crone's making space is a nearby move that the corpus files in a different camp, which bears on how firmly the corpus's five theories separate. |
| Sources | https://www.onecommune.com/blog/the-mind-architect-with-peter-crone; https://www.petercrone.com/benefits-disclaimer |
| Verified at those sources | yes |

| Its own word | What it means |
|---|---|
| *make space* | to let a feeling be present instead of trying to resolve it |
| *feminine or maternal energy* | Crone's name for the accepting stance he takes first, contrasted with a problem-solving stance |

### Reflecting a client's phrasing as perception, not truth (a plain description; the coach gives it no name)

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Type | a move |
| When the coach uses it | Crone uses the move when a client states a judgment about someone or something as a fact. |
| What the coach does | Crone repeats back the words a client used and tells them the phrasing is inaccurate: a statement such as 'he has a wicked slider' is a perception, not a truth, since another batter who hit well against the same pitcher would describe the same pitch differently. He concludes that what the statement reveals is the speaker rather than the pitcher, and that 'what you're only ever experiencing is you.' |
| Steps | (1) The client describes a situation in fixed terms, such as 'he has a wicked slider.' (2) Crone reflects the words back and says: 'the way you phrase them is inaccurate.' (3) Crone offers an observer for whom the opposite description would be true. (4) Crone concludes that the description reveals the speaker, not the thing described. |
| What it does | The move has the client hear their description as their own perception. |
| What is facilitated | The move makes it easier for the client to hear their own description as a view they hold; the coach supplies the correction. |
| Who decides | D, the facilitator directs or interprets with authority. Crone tells the client that their wording is inaccurate, so the judgment of what is true comes from him. |
| The evidence | No published study of Crone's method was found; the support offered is his own account and client testimonials, and his site states that he 'does not claim to diagnose or treat any specific conditions.' |
| How it bears on the book (the researcher's reading) | Who decides the content: Crone rules on which of the client's statements are true, which places that judgment with the coach. |
| Where it differs from the book (the researcher's reading) | The book holds that the reader decides what is true of their experience, while this move has the coach declare a client's description inaccurate. |
| Quoted from its sources | "the way you phrase them is inaccurate. He has a wicked slider. That's not a truth. That's your perception." (https://www.onecommune.com/blog/the-mind-architect-with-peter-crone) "they're nonetheless still caught up in the linguistic limitations of their own perceived ideas of themselves." (https://www.onecommune.com/blog/the-mind-architect-with-peter-crone) |
| Date | Stated in an interview published by Commune on 12 June 2020 |
| In the vault | `notes/the transformation field divides into five theories of where suffering lives.md`; The vault has no record of this move. |
| Sources | https://www.onecommune.com/blog/the-mind-architect-with-peter-crone; https://www.petercrone.com/benefits-disclaimer |
| Verified at those sources | yes |

| Its own word | What it means |
|---|---|
| *perception versus truth* | Crone's distinction between how something seems to a person and what is so |
| *what you're only ever experiencing is you* | Crone's claim that a person's description of the world reveals the one describing it |

### Awareness, then practice

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Type | a technique |
| When the coach uses it | Crone uses this sequence across a coaching engagement, from the first session to the months after. |
| What the coach does | Crone describes his work in two stages. The first, awareness, brings the unconscious belief into view and removes it; the second, practice, is being the person no longer confined by the old narrative, which he says needs repetition. He rejects affirmations used instead of the first stage, as 'compensatory' statements that paper over a belief the person still holds, but accepts them after the belief has been removed, as reminders such as 'I am free. I am loved.' His paid formats include a one-week private 'Inception Week', a three-month retainer with weekly calls and text access, and group masterminds. |
| Steps | (1) Awareness: Crone traces the problem to a belief and the person investigates whether it is true. (2) The person sees that the belief is not a truth. (3) Practice: the person repeatedly acts as someone no longer confined by the old narrative. (4) After the belief is gone, the person may use affirmations as reminders of what they have seen. |
| What it does | The sequence has the person first see through a belief and then practice living without it. |
| What is facilitated | The sequence makes it easier for a person to live without a belief they have seen through, by practicing that life; the person does the practice. |
| Who decides | B, the facilitator shapes the process; the people supply the content |
| The evidence | No published study of Crone's method was found; the support offered is his own account and client testimonials, and his site states that he 'does not claim to diagnose or treat any specific conditions.' |
| How it bears on the book (the researcher's reading) | Remaining the author of one's own change: The second stage is the client's own repeated practice rather than the coach's intervention. Knowing that something happened: Crone uses affirmations as a test of sequence: before the belief is removed they are 'whipped cream' on an unaddressed problem, afterward they are reminders. |
| Where it differs from the book (the researcher's reading) | The book treats lasting change as the result of participation over time within supporting conditions, which is close to Crone's second stage; it does not share his first stage's reliance on a coach's diagnosis. |
| Quoted from its sources | "So, practice, being the person who's no longer confined by the previous narrative that was confining you" (https://miraclemorning.com/peter-crone/) "So, I think there's a place for affirmations, but it's after we freed somebody from the constraints of their subconscious." (https://miraclemorning.com/peter-crone/) "So, it's compensatory." (https://miraclemorning.com/peter-crone/) "oh, I am free. I am loved. I am more than enough" (https://miraclemorning.com/peter-crone/) "that's the sort of the whipped cream on sh*t to me" (https://miraclemorning.com/peter-crone/) "Inception Week is a one-week, private coaching experience designed to reveal and dissolve the patterns shaping results in life, work, health, and relationships." (https://www.petercrone.com/coaching) "The 3-Month Retainer program is designed to provide personalized coaching and guidance to help individuals transition from ego-based living to soul-based living" (https://www.petercrone.com/coaching) |
| Date | Stated in an interview published on Hal Elrod's Achieve Your Goals podcast page, 9 July 2025 |
| In the vault | `notes/tony robbins.md`; The Robbins note records that a changed state does not last unless the underlying structure changes; Crone reaches the same order, belief first and repetition second, by another route. |
| Sources | https://miraclemorning.com/peter-crone/; https://www.petercrone.com/coaching; https://www.petercrone.com/benefits-disclaimer |
| Verified at those sources | yes |

| Its own word | What it means |
|---|---|
| *awareness* | the first stage, bringing an unconscious belief into view |
| *practice* | the second stage, living repeatedly as someone without the belief |
| *affirmations* | repeated positive statements, which Crone accepts only after the belief beneath them has been removed |

### Humor and tough love

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Type | a move |
| When the coach uses it | Crone uses the move with performers and athletes he has coached for some time, when worry about results is getting in their way. |
| What the coach does | Crone says he uses a lot of humor because people take their difficulties too seriously, and that with athletes he has worked with for a while he uses 'tough love' to stop them worrying about outcomes that have not happened. A typical reversal answers a client who wishes they did not care so much: Crone tells them they do not care enough, because they are 'worrying more than you're caring', and redirects them toward dedication to their craft. |
| Steps | (1) Crone uses humor to lighten a client's seriousness about their problem. (2) When a client says 'I wish I didn't care so much', Crone answers: 'no, you don't care enough.' (3) Crone separates worry about outcomes from care for the craft. (4) With long-term clients Crone confronts worry bluntly as 'tough love'. |
| What it does | The move has the coach bluntly redirect a client from worry about outcomes to care for the work. |
| What is facilitated | The move makes it easier for a client to drop worry about outcomes and put their effort into the work; the coach supplies the confrontation. |
| Who decides | D, the facilitator directs or interprets with authority |
| The evidence | No published study of Crone's method was found; the support offered is his own account and client testimonials, and his site states that he 'does not claim to diagnose or treat any specific conditions.' |
| How it bears on the book (the researcher's reading) | Timing and pacing: Crone reserves tough love for clients he has worked with for a while, after acceptance has been established. Who decides the content: The reversal replaces the client's own description ('I wish I didn't care so much') with the coach's. |
| Where it differs from the book (the researcher's reading) | The book's prose does not confront its reader, while Crone relies on blunt reversal with established clients. |
| Quoted from its sources | "I use a lot of humor in my work" (https://www.onecommune.com/blog/the-mind-architect-with-peter-crone) "And this is where I bring in a lot of tough love with the athletes" (https://www.onecommune.com/blog/the-mind-architect-with-peter-crone) "I say, no, you don't care enough. Because you're actually worrying more than you're caring." (https://www.onecommune.com/blog/the-mind-architect-with-peter-crone) "I wish I didn't care so much." (https://www.onecommune.com/blog/the-mind-architect-with-peter-crone) |
| Date | Stated in an interview published by Commune on 12 June 2020 |
| In the vault | `notes/the transformation field divides into five theories of where suffering lives.md`; The vault has no record of this move. |
| Sources | https://www.onecommune.com/blog/the-mind-architect-with-peter-crone; https://www.petercrone.com/benefits-disclaimer |
| Verified at those sources | yes |

| Its own word | What it means |
|---|---|
| *tough love* | Crone's term for blunt confrontation of a client's worry |
| *worry versus care* | Crone's distinction between anxiety about outcomes and dedication to the work itself |

## David Deida

| Move | Type | When the coach uses it | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| Closure and opening, from the edge to zero | a theory | Deida teaches this account in his books, workshops, and recordings. | The account names closure against hurt and fear as the source of suffering, and opening, later non-resistance, as the practice. |
| Knowing your real edge and leaning just beyond it | a move | Deida assigns it to men, with a weekly circle of men friends as witnesses. | The move has a man find where he holds back out of fear and act just past that point, with friends who assign and check the act. |
| Practicing love when hurt | a move | Deida teaches it for moments when a partner acts unlovingly. | The move has a hurt person keep their heart open instead of withdrawing or retaliating. |
| Unclench as if asleep (Blue Truth, chapter 9) | a technique | Deida offers it for waking stress and for any moment of the day. | The practice has a reader sink inward as if falling asleep, find a silent and fearless depth, and open the eyes while keeping it. |
| The Dollar exercise (Instant Enlightenment, chapter 18) | a technique | Deida offers it to readers who want to find their blocks around sex and money. | The exercise has a reader imagine a small rule-breaking gift and notice the charge it raises. |
| Breathing sexual energy in a circle (The Enlightened Sex Manual, chapters 27 and 28) | a technique | The manual teaches it for solo practice first and then for couples during sex. | The practice uses breath and a lift of the pelvic floor to circulate arousal up the spine and down the front of the body instead of releasing it. |
| Staying at zero with a partner's upset | a move | Deida teaches it for moments when a partner comes with upset or criticism and the more masculine partner feels a flinch. | The move has a person rest in the awareness present before, during, and after the encounter, then feel the partner's emotion as energy and the shared wish for love beneath it. |
| Witnessing inherited patterns | a move | Deida teaches it for thoughts or urges a person does not endorse, such as violent thoughts or addictive tendencies. | The move has a person watch an inherited impulse pass without resisting it, acting on it, or feeling ashamed of it. |
| Riverbanking | a move | Deida teaches it for moments when a partner is undecided among many directions. | The move offers a concrete option to an undecided partner without requiring that they take it. |

### Closure and opening, from the edge to zero

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Type | a theory |
| When the coach uses it | Deida teaches this account in his books, workshops, and recordings. |
| What the coach does | In his first book Deida defines love as 'simply what is when your heart is open' and says a person closes off to love when they guard the heart; the foundation of his practice is 'to practice opening your heart in every moment, including when you feel hurt'. Blue Truth says 'Fear underlies most of your life'. The Way of the Superior Man (1997), read by the vault, defines suffering as resisting the giving of one's gift. In 2026 Deida says that book 'was a way of living at my edge', and that his new book describes a man past personal purpose whose practice is 'zero', which he defines as 'no resistance': 'When you no longer resist what is, when you’re no longer trying to add something to what is or subtract from what is—that’s zero.' |
| What it does | The account names closure against hurt and fear as the source of suffering, and opening, later non-resistance, as the practice. |
| What is facilitated | The practice makes it more likely that a person opens heart, body, and attention under stress or hurt; the opening is the person's. Deida supplies instructions, exercises, and a doctrine of masculine and feminine. |
| Who decides | D, the facilitator directs or interprets with authority. Deida states his doctrine with authority in 'black-and-white' terms and asks readers to adapt it to their situation. |
| The evidence | Deida's account rests on his teaching; his website's copy speaks of '30 years of work with thousands of couples', and the sources opened report no study. Wikipedia's article on him displays warnings that it relies on self-published sources. |
| How it bears on the book (the researcher's reading) | Protection and defense: Deida reads suffering as closure against hurt and fear, the same shape as the book's contraction, and asks for opening even while hurt. Recognition: In 2026 he describes finding 'a beingness that’s there all the time' and relaxing as it, which is recognition in his words. |
| Where it differs from the book (the researcher's reading) | The corpus's fifth theory, a closed body or a withheld gift, fits the 1997 book; Deida's 2026 account moves to non-resistance, nearer the corpus's first and second theories. He keeps a gendered doctrine throughout, writing, he says, in a 'black-and-white way' that he asks readers to adapt. |
| Limits and critiques | Tracy Clark-Flory (Jezebel, 14 September 2021) calls the argument of The Way of the Superior Man 'fundamentally generalizing and essentialist' and says its framework 'is easily used to justify men as the rightful patriarchs of the world'. Wikipedia's article records Joseph Gelfer's allegation of misogyny in one of his books (Numen, Old Men, 2009, p. 121) and a critic in EnlightenNext who questioned his spiritual depth and integrity. Jayson Gaddis, a relationship coach who attended one of Deida's five-day intensives, wrote in 2010 that only months later, talking with his wife, did he realize he had crossed some boundaries during it, and that Deida's work can put some men in a double bind between being told to open their partner and being blamed as unmanly for relaxing. Deida himself says that presence does not stop inherited contractions from arising, and he refers people with trauma to somatic releasing techniques and family constellation work. |
| Quoted from its sources | "David Deida’s first book lays the foundation for his teaching on the integration of intimacy and authentic spiritual practice." (https://deida.info/free-chapters/intimate-communion/) "Love is simply what is when your heart is open." (https://daviddeida-freechapters.s3.us-east-2.amazonaws.com/Intimate-Communion/2_Untangling_Love.pdf) "exist as love when your heart is unguarded and opened, and you close yourself off to love when you guard your heart." (https://daviddeida-freechapters.s3.us-east-2.amazonaws.com/Intimate-Communion/2_Untangling_Love.pdf) "This is the foundation of Intimate Communion: to practice opening your heart in every moment, including when you feel hurt." (https://daviddeida-freechapters.s3.us-east-2.amazonaws.com/Intimate-Communion/2_Untangling_Love.pdf) "Fear underlies most of your life." (https://daviddeida-freechapters.s3.us-east-2.amazonaws.com/Blue-Truth/BK00908-BlueTruth-ch9.pdf) "Yes, it is true to say that for me, The Way of the Superior Man was a way of living at my edge." (https://resources2.soundstrue.com/transcript/david-deida-the-way-of-zero/) "Zero is no resistance." (https://resources2.soundstrue.com/transcript/david-deida-the-way-of-zero/) "When you no longer resist what is, when you’re no longer trying to add something to what is or subtract from what is—that’s zero." (https://resources2.soundstrue.com/transcript/david-deida-the-way-of-zero/) "There’s a beingness that’s there all the time." (https://resources2.soundstrue.com/transcript/david-deida-the-way-of-zero/) "I write in a kind of black-and-white way, mostly because it’s easier for me" (https://resources2.soundstrue.com/transcript/david-deida-the-way-of-zero/) "The universal feminine is constantly testing the masculine" (https://resources2.soundstrue.com/transcript/david-deida-the-way-of-zero/) "There are very specific techniques you could use—if you have trauma in the body, somatic releasing techniques; if you have trauma inherited through family patterns, family constellation work." (https://resources2.soundstrue.com/transcript/david-deida-the-way-of-zero/) "You were born as a sacrifice. And you can either participate in the sacrifice, dissolving in the giving of your gift, or you can resist it, which is your suffering" (archive/reading — robbins and deida in their own words (awaken the giant within, unlimited power, the way of the superior man).md) "Deida’s argument is fundamentally generalizing and essentialist." (https://www.jezebel.com/crazy-women-fiery-latinas-and-masculine-sexual-essenc-1847495117) "Deida claims he believes in equality, but the book’s framework is easily used to justify men as the rightful patriarchs of the world." (https://www.jezebel.com/crazy-women-fiery-latinas-and-masculine-sexual-essenc-1847495117) "September 14, 2021" (https://www.jezebel.com/crazy-women-fiery-latinas-and-masculine-sexual-essenc-1847495117) "officially recommends the book" (https://www.jezebel.com/crazy-women-fiery-latinas-and-masculine-sexual-essenc-1847495117) "From 1986 to 1988, Deida studied with the American spiritual teacher" (https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Deida) "is one of the many founding associates at the Integral Institute" (https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Deida) "Other criticism questions Deida's political stance, alleging an element of misogyny in one of his books." (https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Deida) "Gelfer, Joseph; Numen, Old Men: Contemporary Masculine Spiritualities and the Problem of Patriarchy, 2009, p. 121." (https://www.jaysongaddis.com/the-straightjacket-of-david-deida/) "And, it wasn’t until months later in discussing the workshop with my wife, that I realized I had crossed some boundaries during the intensive." (https://www.jaysongaddis.com/the-straightjacket-of-david-deida/) "Without knowing it, Deida’s work can put some men in a double bind." (https://www.jaysongaddis.com/the-straightjacket-of-david-deida/) "On the other hand, if they decide to become real, truthful, and relax into “being” they face getting blamed wimpy, feminine, or called out (in their men’s group or by their woman) for not being man enough." (https://www.jaysongaddis.com/the-straightjacket-of-david-deida/) "presents an explanation of sexual polarity in relation to" (https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sexual_polarity) |
| Date | 1995 (Intimate Communion) to 2026 (The Man of Zero) |
| In the vault | archive/reading — robbins and deida in their own words (awaken the giant within, unlimited power, the way of the superior man).md; The reading record grounds the fifth theory in Deida's 1997 text ('you can resist it, which is your suffering'). His 2026 interview presents that book as a stage, 'a way of living at my edge', and names 'no resistance' as the practice, which the vault does not yet hold. |

| Its own word | What it means |
|---|---|
| *closure* | Closure is Deida's word for guarding the heart and contracting the body against hurt or fear. |
| *edge* | The edge is the point at which a person stops short of their fullest gift out of fear. |
| *zero* | Zero, in Deida's 2026 book, is no resistance to what is, which he distinguishes from having no thoughts or feelings. |
| *polarity* | Polarity is the attraction Deida says arises between a masculine and a feminine pole in any couple. |

### Knowing your real edge and leaning just beyond it

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Type | a move |
| When the coach uses it | Deida assigns it to men, with a weekly circle of men friends as witnesses. |
| What the coach does | The vault's reading of The Way of the Superior Man records the edge as 'where you stop short, or where you compromise your fullest gift, and, instead, cater to your fears', and the instruction to stay with fear at the edge. It also records weekly meetings with close men friends who give 'a behavioral experiment' and an agreed consequence 'for freezing in the face of fear'. In 2026 Deida described that book as 'a way of stretching myself and feeling how I can serve others, how I could give others the gift so that their lives are better—and how do I challenge myself so I’m always growing, one step at a time from where I’m at.' He adds that after living this way for a while a man may lose his former purpose, and warns that 'A man can collapse when he’s feeling unmotivated.' |
| Steps | (1) The man locates his edge: where he stops short, compromises his fullest gift, and caters to his fears. (2) He leans just beyond it and stays with the fear. (3) About once a week he meets his closest men friends, who give him a behavioral experiment. (4) They agree on a consequence for freezing in the face of fear. (5) He challenges himself, in Deida's 2026 words, 'one step at a time from where I’m at'. |
| What it does | The move has a man find where he holds back out of fear and act just past that point, with friends who assign and check the act. |
| What is facilitated | The move makes it more likely that a man acts just past his fear; the act is his, while his friends supply the assignment, the witnessing, and the consequence. |
| Who decides | D, the facilitator directs or interprets with authority. Deida instructs the reader, and in his scheme a man's friends assign the act and the consequence. |
| The evidence | The vault read the 1997 text at Grade A; the 2026 wording comes from Deida's interview transcript. The sources opened contain no study. |
| How it bears on the book (the researcher's reading) | Participation rather than watching: The move requires an act at a threshold, assigned and witnessed, not reflection alone. Timing and pacing: Deida's 2026 wording, 'one step at a time from where I’m at', sets the pace as small steps just past the edge. |
| Where it differs from the book (the researcher's reading) | Deida now presents the edge practice as a stage that can end in the loss of purpose he calls zero, while the corpus treats 'give your gift at your edge' as his axiom. |
| Limits and critiques | The vault's reading records the gendered grounds Deida gives for the witnesses, among them 'A man’s capacity to receive another man’s direct criticism is a measure of his capacity to receive masculine energy.' Jayson Gaddis, a former participant, wrote in 2010 that readers of Deida often 'begin to “perform” to get the technique right'. |
| Quoted from its sources | "Your edge is where you stop short, or where you compromise your fullest gift, and, instead, cater to your fears" (archive/reading — robbins and deida in their own words (awaken the giant within, unlimited power, the way of the superior man).md) "Staying with the fear, staying at your edge, allows real transformation to occur" (archive/reading — robbins and deida in their own words (awaken the giant within, unlimited power, the way of the superior man).md) "Then, your friends should give you a behavioral experiment, something you can do that will reveal something to you" (archive/reading — robbins and deida in their own words (awaken the giant within, unlimited power, the way of the superior man).md) "there must be a consequence for freezing in the face of fear" (archive/reading — robbins and deida in their own words (awaken the giant within, unlimited power, the way of the superior man).md) "A man’s capacity to receive another man’s direct criticism is a measure of his capacity to receive masculine energy." (archive/reading — robbins and deida in their own words (awaken the giant within, unlimited power, the way of the superior man).md) "It was a way of stretching myself and feeling how I can serve others, how I could give others the gift so that their lives are better—and how do I challenge myself so I’m always growing, one step at a time from where I’m at." (https://resources2.soundstrue.com/transcript/david-deida-the-way-of-zero/) "A man can collapse when he’s feeling unmotivated." (https://resources2.soundstrue.com/transcript/david-deida-the-way-of-zero/) "readers of Deida begin to “perform” to get the technique right" (https://www.jaysongaddis.com/the-straightjacket-of-david-deida/) |
| Date | 1997 (The Way of the Superior Man, chs. 4, 8, 10, and 50); Deida's 2026 description of it |
| In the vault | archive/reading — robbins and deida in their own words (awaken the giant within, unlimited power, the way of the superior man).md; The reading record holds the edge, the weekly circle, and the consequence for freezing at Grade A, with the gendered grounds for the witnesses; this entry adds only the 2026 wording. |
| Sources | https://resources2.soundstrue.com/transcript/david-deida-the-way-of-zero/; https://resources2.soundstrue.com/podcast/david-deida-the-way-of-zero/; https://www.jaysongaddis.com/the-straightjacket-of-david-deida/ |
| Verified at those sources | yes |

| Its own word | What it means |
|---|---|
| *edge* | The edge is where a man stops short of his fullest gift and caters to his fears. |
| *behavioral experiment* | A behavioral experiment is an act a man's friends assign him, meant to reveal something to him. |

### Practicing love when hurt

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Type | a move |
| When the coach uses it | Deida teaches it for moments when a partner acts unlovingly. |
| What the coach does | Intimate Communion asks partners to practice opening the heart 'in every moment, including when you feel hurt', rather than turning away, closing down, or punishing the partner. Deida grants that some looking into childhood is good, and then says that 'after a little digging, it is time to release' the past and practice intimacy in the present. He compares childhood wounds to a knee that heals if it is not hurt again, and says that through this practice 'our hurt does not become closure'. He adds that this practice of love extends beyond conventional therapy. |
| Steps | (1) The person notices the hurt when a partner acts unlovingly. (2) The person notices the impulse to turn away, close down, become distant, or punish the partner. (3) The person practices loving instead and keeps the heart open; Deida writes, 'Rather than turn away or close down, you can practice loving.' (4) The person keeps attention on the present relationship rather than on the past cause. |
| What it does | The move has a hurt person keep their heart open instead of withdrawing or retaliating. |
| What is facilitated | The move makes it more likely that a hurt partner stays open and that old patterns of closing down weaken; the choice is the reader's. |
| Who decides | E, no live facilitator; a structure, text, or custom does the work. The book gives the instruction, and the reader practices it in their own relationship. |
| The evidence | The source is a chapter of Deida's book; the sources opened contain no study. |
| How it bears on the book (the researcher's reading) | Protection and defense: The move names closing down after hurt as the protective habit and asks the person to keep the heart open instead. Conflict: The move addresses the moment when one partner acts unlovingly and the other is tempted to strike back. |
| Where it differs from the book (the researcher's reading) | The book reads a contraction as something to learn from; Deida sets aside most inquiry into causes in favor of opening now, and he places his practice beyond therapy. |
| Quoted from its sources | "Rather than turn away or close down, you can practice loving." (https://daviddeida-freechapters.s3.us-east-2.amazonaws.com/Intimate-Communion/2_Untangling_Love.pdf) "But after a little digging, it is time to release" (https://daviddeida-freechapters.s3.us-east-2.amazonaws.com/Intimate-Communion/2_Untangling_Love.pdf) "In the same way, your childhood wounds will heal on their own, as long as you don’t repeat the old pattern of wounding yourself over and over again." (https://daviddeida-freechapters.s3.us-east-2.amazonaws.com/Intimate-Communion/2_Untangling_Love.pdf) "may still feel hurt when our partner acts unlovingly, but our hurt does not become closure." (https://daviddeida-freechapters.s3.us-east-2.amazonaws.com/Intimate-Communion/2_Untangling_Love.pdf) |
| Date | 1995 (Intimate Communion, ch. 2, 'Untangling Love') |
| In the vault | `notes/the third entity's failure mode is withdrawal and its core skill is staying.md`; The vault holds that the relational field's core skill is staying; Deida's instruction to practice loving instead of closing down when hurt states the same skill as heart-opening. |
| Sources | https://daviddeida-freechapters.s3.us-east-2.amazonaws.com/Intimate-Communion/2_Untangling_Love.pdf; https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Deida |
| Verified at those sources | yes |

| Its own word | What it means |
|---|---|
| *closure* | Closure is guarding the heart after a hurt, which Deida says creates distance in a relationship. |
| *Intimate Communion* | Intimate Communion is Deida's name for the practice of open-hearted intimacy in every moment. |

### Unclench as if asleep (Blue Truth, chapter 9)

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Type | a technique |
| When the coach uses it | Deida offers it for waking stress and for any moment of the day. |
| What the coach does | The chapter observes that there is no fear in deep, dreamless sleep, while in waking life fear 'keeps your mind on the waves'. It asks the reader to stay sensitive as they wake, to feel the silent peace beneath emerging thoughts, and to discover that 'in any moment, you can feel into the province of sleep’s deep openness, even now.' The practice is to close the eyes, sink down and inward as if going to sleep, feel the silent, boundless, stress-free place within, relax into it, and then open the eyes. Deida says that 'With practice, you can relax as the source of sleep’s depth even while awake.' |
| Steps | (1) The reader closes the eyes for a moment and sinks down and inward, as if going to sleep. (2) The reader feels for 'the place deep within that is silent, boundless, and utterly stress-free'. (3) The reader relaxes into this place 'as if you were easing into the deepest sleep'. (4) When the reader feels this silent depth, as if deep asleep, they open their eyes. (5) On waking each day, the reader remains sensitive while emerging from deep sleep into wakeful stress. |
| What it does | The practice has a reader sink inward as if falling asleep, find a silent and fearless depth, and open the eyes while keeping it. |
| What is facilitated | The practice makes it more likely that a reader feels a fearless depth while awake; the practice and the finding are the reader's own. |
| Who decides | E, no live facilitator; a structure, text, or custom does the work. The book gives the instructions and the reader does the practice alone. |
| The evidence | The source is a chapter of Deida's book; the sources opened contain no study. |
| How it bears on the book (the researcher's reading) | Recognition: The practice points to a depth present beneath waking experience and asks the reader to 'relax as the source', a recognition practice written for a reader alone. Who decides the content: The text supplies only the instructions, and the reader finds the depth, or does not, in their own experience. |
| Where it differs from the book (the researcher's reading) | The practice is written for a reader alone, like the book, and points to what is already present; it differs in taking deep sleep's absence of fear as the reference state. |
| Quoted from its sources | "deep, dreamless sleep, there is no fear" (https://daviddeida-freechapters.s3.us-east-2.amazonaws.com/Blue-Truth/BK00908-BlueTruth-ch9.pdf) "fear keeps your mind on the waves." (https://daviddeida-freechapters.s3.us-east-2.amazonaws.com/Blue-Truth/BK00908-BlueTruth-ch9.pdf) "in any moment, you can feel into the province of sleep’s deep openness, even now." (https://daviddeida-freechapters.s3.us-east-2.amazonaws.com/Blue-Truth/BK00908-BlueTruth-ch9.pdf) "feel the place deep within that is silent, boundless, and utterly stress-free." (https://daviddeida-freechapters.s3.us-east-2.amazonaws.com/Blue-Truth/BK00908-BlueTruth-ch9.pdf) "Relax into this place as if you were easing into the deepest sleep." (https://daviddeida-freechapters.s3.us-east-2.amazonaws.com/Blue-Truth/BK00908-BlueTruth-ch9.pdf) "When you feel this silent depth of blissful feeling, as if deep asleep, then open your eyes." (https://daviddeida-freechapters.s3.us-east-2.amazonaws.com/Blue-Truth/BK00908-BlueTruth-ch9.pdf) "With practice, you can relax as the source of sleep’s depth even while awake." (https://daviddeida-freechapters.s3.us-east-2.amazonaws.com/Blue-Truth/BK00908-BlueTruth-ch9.pdf) "Remain sensitive as you emerge from deep sleep into wakeful stress." (https://daviddeida-freechapters.s3.us-east-2.amazonaws.com/Blue-Truth/BK00908-BlueTruth-ch9.pdf) |

### The Dollar exercise (Instant Enlightenment, chapter 18)

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Type | a technique |
| When the coach uses it | Deida offers it to readers who want to find their blocks around sex and money. |
| What the coach does | Deida asks the reader to imagine going to a public place with friends with whom they feel safe, choosing 'a trustworthy-looking stranger whom you find sexy', giving that person a dollar, and saying a scripted sentence that ends contact. The reader then notices how it feels to give money while calling someone sexy, since 'Most of us are complicated about both sex and money.' Deida says the best way to find where 'sexual obstructions and financial blocks reside within you' is to break the usual rules in the least risky ways, and asks the reader to repeat this or other exercises of imagined gifts beyond the comfort zone. |
| Steps | (1) The reader imagines going to a public place with a group of friends with whom they feel most safe. (2) The reader imagines choosing, with the friends, a trustworthy-looking stranger whom they find sexy. (3) The reader imagines giving the stranger a dollar and saying: 'I’m doing this as part of an assigned exercise. I’m supposed to give a dollar to somebody really sexy. Then I am to have no further contact with you.' (4) The reader notices how it feels to give someone a dollar while saying they are sexy. (5) The reader repeats the exercise or others that require imagining 'offering gifts beyond your comfort zone'. (6) The reader asks the chapter's questions, among them 'Are you willing to face, feel, confess, and open beyond your every sexual and financial fear and complication now?' |
| What it does | The exercise has a reader imagine a small rule-breaking gift and notice the charge it raises. |
| What is facilitated | The exercise makes a reader's own blocks around sex and money more visible through an imagined risk; the reaction is the reader's. |
| Who decides | E, no live facilitator; a structure, text, or custom does the work. The book gives the imagined scenario, and the reader notices their own reaction. |
| The evidence | The source is a chapter of Deida's book; the sources opened contain no study. |
| How it bears on the book (the researcher's reading) | Protection and defense: The exercise treats hidden sexual and financial fears as 'refusals to open as love' and uses an imagined risk to find them. Harm and its conditions: If acted out rather than imagined, the exercise would involve a stranger who has not agreed to be part of it. |
| Where it differs from the book (the researcher's reading) | The book does not ask its reader to act toward strangers; Deida's exercise uses an imagined social risk to reveal a reader's blocks. |
| Limits and critiques | The chapter asks the reader to imagine the exercise; acted out, it would involve a stranger who has not agreed to take part. |
| Quoted from its sources | "Imagine going to a public place with a group of friends. Pick friends with whom you feel most safe." (https://daviddeida-freechapters.s3.us-east-2.amazonaws.com/Instant-Enlightenment/BK01123-InstantEnlighten-ch18.pdf) "imagine locating a trustworthy-looking stranger whom you find sexy." (https://daviddeida-freechapters.s3.us-east-2.amazonaws.com/Instant-Enlightenment/BK01123-InstantEnlighten-ch18.pdf) "“I’m doing this as part of an assigned exercise. I’m supposed to give a dollar to somebody really sexy. Then I am to have no further contact with you.”" (https://daviddeida-freechapters.s3.us-east-2.amazonaws.com/Instant-Enlightenment/BK01123-InstantEnlighten-ch18.pdf) "Most of us are complicated about both sex and money." (https://daviddeida-freechapters.s3.us-east-2.amazonaws.com/Instant-Enlightenment/BK01123-InstantEnlighten-ch18.pdf) "to break the usual rules in the least risky ways, in order to find out where sexual obstructions and financial blocks reside within you." (https://daviddeida-freechapters.s3.us-east-2.amazonaws.com/Instant-Enlightenment/BK01123-InstantEnlighten-ch18.pdf) "Repeat this exercise, or do others that require you to imagine offering gifts beyond your comfort zone." (https://daviddeida-freechapters.s3.us-east-2.amazonaws.com/Instant-Enlightenment/BK01123-InstantEnlighten-ch18.pdf) "These secrets are among your most hidden refusals to open as love." (https://daviddeida-freechapters.s3.us-east-2.amazonaws.com/Instant-Enlightenment/BK01123-InstantEnlighten-ch18.pdf) "Are you willing to face, feel, confess, and open beyond your every sexual and financial fear and complication now?"
