| Its own word | What it means |
|---|---|
| *conscious warriorhood* | Conscious warriorhood is Wineland's term for making one's body and presence an instrument of consciousness and using one's fierceness well. |

### Offering recognition as nourishment (a plain description; the coach gives it no name)

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Type | a move |
| When the coach uses it | Wineland teaches it to partners in workshops and uses archetypes to help them find it. |
| What the coach does | Wineland says that in workshops men who receive 'devotional energy' from a partner often weep, and that what nourishes the masculine in anyone is 'recognition through the body': being recognized for the good they try to do and the stands they make. For the feminine in anyone he names the nourishment 'to be grounded, seen, felt in the truth of her heart'. He works with 'archetypes and textures' to help partners find these, and he insists 'It’s not something that you have to do to be loved. It’s not something that you need to weaponize.' When his interviewer summarized that 'what we all want, actually, is a type of recognition and being seen', he agreed. |
| Steps | (1) Partners learn which kinds of energy nourish each other, with the help of archetypes and textures. (2) A partner offers recognition through the body to the masculine in the other, for the good they are trying to do and the stands they make. (3) A partner offers the feminine in the other what Wineland describes as being 'grounded, seen, felt in the truth of her heart'. (4) Partners offer these as gifts, not as conditions for love and not as weapons. |
| What it does | The move has a partner offer, through the body, the specific recognition the other most needs. |
| What is facilitated | The move makes it more likely that a person feels recognized by a partner; the recognition is received in the person's own body and on their own terms. |
| Who decides | C, the facilitator offers content as an option. Wineland names kinds of nourishment, and partners decide what to offer each other. |
| The evidence | The source is Wineland's interview; the sources opened contain no study. |
| How it bears on the book (the researcher's reading) | Being seen and recognized by another: Wineland describes men weeping when recognized 'through the body', which is the effect of being seen by another. Harm and its conditions: Wineland warns that people 'weaponize these terms all the time', which names the misuse of polarity language in relationships. |
| Where it differs from the book (the researcher's reading) | Wineland divides the needed recognition into kinds for the masculine and the feminine, while the book's recognition is a person coming to know what they already are, not nourishment supplied by a partner. |
| Limits and critiques | The gendered division of what partners need is the point that Clark-Flory's critique of Deida's framework contests; Wineland himself warns against weaponizing the terms. |
| Quoted from its sources | "when a man is receiving, let’s call it devotional energy, from a partner, there’s an almost… Many times they’ll burst into tears." (https://resources2.soundstrue.com/transcript/masculine-depth-and-power-from-the-core/) "To be recognized for the good that they are trying to do, to be recognized for the stands that they are trying to make, to be recognized as a good human who fights for love, fights for consciousness, protects his family, tries to, that recognition through the body is a major piece of nourishment." (https://resources2.soundstrue.com/transcript/masculine-depth-and-power-from-the-core/) "which is to be grounded, seen, felt in the truth of her heart." (https://resources2.soundstrue.com/transcript/masculine-depth-and-power-from-the-core/) "It’s not something that you have to do to be loved. It’s not something that you need to weaponize." (https://resources2.soundstrue.com/transcript/masculine-depth-and-power-from-the-core/) "We work a lot with archetypes and textures because these are just easy ways to access those pieces of nourishment." (https://resources2.soundstrue.com/transcript/masculine-depth-and-power-from-the-core/) "what we all want, actually, is a type of recognition and being seen" (https://resources2.soundstrue.com/transcript/masculine-depth-and-power-from-the-core/) "Yes, that’s a beautiful way to put it. I think that’s very true." (https://resources2.soundstrue.com/transcript/masculine-depth-and-power-from-the-core/) |
| Date | 2022 (From the Core: A New Masculine Paradigm for Leading with Love, Living Your Truth, and Healing the World, Sounds True; Insights at the Edge interview with Tami Simon, 2 August 2022) |

|---|---|

| *archetypes and textures* | Archetypes and textures are the named qualities of energy, such as the magical, that Wineland uses to help partners find what nourishes each other. |
